mr president, commissioner, president-in-office of the council, today the european parliament is taking another step towards protecting the health of europeans. covid-19 has taught us many things, and the main one is that microbes know no borders between countries. coordinated work within and outside the european union between member states and international agencies such as the world health organisation is therefore urgently needed. this report is also another step towards reducing social inequalities and ensuring the equitable distribution of medicines, vaccines, medical devices and protective equipment during health crises. this pandemic has also been a real stress test for our health systems. what we want with this legislation is for these tests to be done in a controlled way so that we can identify the flaws and correct them in time. the key to being prepared for future threats also lies in training health and social services professionals and in strengthening the health literacy of citizens, for citizens, so that they can better understand the directions of health authorities. we also need robust information systems, from data collection to data analysis and interpretation, taking full advantage of the new digital age to reduce the administrative burden and duplication of resources in what is a very time-consuming process. the enhancement of the ecdc, its competence and its capacity for rapid risk analysis will result in a greater capacity to mitigate the consequences of future health crises. i want to conclude by reminding you that health is not limited to health systems. health impacts all sectors of our society and requires coordinated work among all to promote health, prevent disease and prepare for crises. this is achieved only by a genuine health approach in all policies and in one health.
mr. chairman, colleagues, commissioner, a bicycle courier is on his way to your house with a pizza, but he has an accident and no insurance. an uber driver is fired on the spot for not being online for a while and is not entitled to benefits. platform workers today have little protection, because they are so-called self-employed. but, colleagues, nothing could be further from the truth. however, all workers in the european union are entitled to equal employment rights and to protection of safety and health at work. at least in theory. our couriers and our caterers are not self-employed. they work for big multinationals. this unequal treatment cannot be justified. i call on the commission to support parliament's position and finally to work towards that equal protection. the updating of the rules on health and safety at work is a unique opportunity to do so. colleagues, we are talking about 24 million people who sometimes go through the motions for their jobs and who deserve protection today. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the european parliament is able to take part in the debate. so let's work together to make it happen.
madam president, i have to ask you to leave. innovative solutions are needed and consumers are also keen to use applications to order services. platform workers also have the right to healthy and safe working conditions, social care, further training and fair and transparent wages. however, they are often self-employed, and care must be taken not to force them to do so, but at the same time the choices of those who wish to work in this way must be respected. and let's not forget that during the covid pandemic, this form of employment has saved many lives, and it's provided many people with income. finally, the interests of smes should be better taken into account, as they are the backbone of the european economy, representing 99% of all eu enterprises. i therefore find it regrettable that the report mentions smes in only two places.
the commission has already made a number of proposals in this area. in recent years, there has been a lot of talk about the fundamental changes in the world of work that have taken place simply and profoundly through digitization and the platform economy. this week, however, we have the opportunity not only to talk about these important changes but also to set a real exclamation point and to make clear to the commission what we expect. in the last few weeks and months, when we were working on your report, dear sylvie, we found some good compromises in the middle of this house which, in my view, if the commission is prepared to include them in its proposal, could be a real game changer, because on the one hand they mean that we are not able to control the dynamics in the platform economy, that the highly qualified people who are self-employed and want to remain self-employed are left out, so to speak, while on the other hand we are now finally, finally providing legal means for all those who have been pushed into virtual self-employment to be given the status and protection they deserve. because what we see in many areas has nothing to do with entrepreneurship. so where an algorithm, so to speak, determines the daily work of people, one cannot speak of true self-employment and true entrepreneurship, but here it is simply and profoundly about passing on entrepreneurial risk to others. and we cannot and will not tolerate such models. and also, we want to have more transparency with the employees about the algorithm. what we have presented here is a real steeple for you, dear nicolas, and that is why i am not only asking for approval, but also really asking for a clear signal. the commission does not accept narrow majorities, but only clear majorities.
madam president, on 6 may a deliveryman lost his life on a bicycle in normandy. after a rainy and cold day, he was delivering a meal under pressure, fatigued and overworked. unfortunately, this drama is not the first, but hopefully it is the last. platform workers have become a rapidly growing part of the transport sector and this is precarious self-employment, especially in driving and delivery services. algorithmic management so often used by platforms can put excessive pressure on workers, with implications for road safety. the covid-19 pandemic clearly shows a lack of social protection for these workers. many of them lost their source of income from one day to another, but could often not access the income measures, neither for employers nor for self-employed. platform workers should enjoy equal access to decent working conditions, including a fair income, social security and protection for unemployment and sickness. work tasks should be predictable and transparent, and algorithms must always have human oversight. those working in the driving and delivery services need adequate protective equipment and accident insurance. we need to ensure that all workers are treated equally in the eu. with this report, the european parliament sends a strong signal in favour of platform workers’ rights. it’s now up to the commission to deliver a progressive legislative proposal.
madam president, only france can decide to amend its own labour code. here's a statement that may not be true tomorrow. this is confirmed by the report on the workers on the platforms. the appalling working conditions and the precariousness of the bicycle delivery men who ride the streets of our cities are all the more indicative of the fact that unbridled globalisation is creating a veritable economic jungle which we must combat. the european union, which is suffering from the syndrome of the savior, is now running to their aid. it was a waste. as always, the brussels bureaucracy, whose slowness in action is no longer to be demonstrated, is outdated: spain, france, but above all switzerland and the united kingdom, outside the union, are already taking action to remedy the problems of false selfemployed. our nations do this by passing laws or by allowing the courts to act sovereignly. the requalification of contracts is under way and that is a good thing. therefore, let us not allow the european union to take advantage of people's misery to meddle in matters which are not within its competence. france is doing and must do more to pay those who must be paid. we also need to end the scandalous use of illegals in the platform economy. we reject immigration which causes social dumping. our jobs should be reserved for our own people first. if globalization is synonymous with precariousness, let us make the nation the lever that will make it possible to fight effectively against these unworthy practices.
madam president, platforms are sold as a magical world of innovation and entrepreneurship. but, but beyond the marketing and the dazzling, the mobile apps, the services that are provided, even the algorithms, are far from being innovations today. the only magic that the platforms offer is the trick of making the boss and the corresponding work relationship disappear. the only innovation they have provided is the ability to travel back in time to the 19th century and the framework of social and labour rights that existed there. the only entrepreneurship that really exists is the transfer of economic risk from the company that hires to the worker who works and risks everything. what we need is to treat platform workers like workers, collective bargaining, employment contracts, social protection. by the way, for those and for all the workers.
madam president, we're here to discuss the working conditions of workers on digital platforms. they may talk about new forms of employment or new realities of the world of work, but the form of exploitation is the same, and old, reminiscent of the day-shift, piecework and waving with freedom in the form of flexibility to increase labour deregulation and precariousness. digital platforms mainly use fake freelancers who often work under the platform's guise and rules and do so for a commission or paying a percentage of the price charged always under the rules determined by the platform. there is no other solution than to recognise the existing employment relationship in accordance with the national legislation of each country. it is unacceptable that in the 21st century the technical and scientific progress achieved should not be used to improve the living and working conditions of workers, but rather to increase their exploitation.
madam president, commissioner, today, as always in history, the socialists defend the rights of the workers, especially the most vulnerable. there is an army of people working through digital platforms today, and the overwhelming majority of them are not entitled to a pension or sick leave, working an average of 60 hours a week. this lack of protection and precariousness is disguised as independent work, when we all know that the relationship is one of dependence, often extreme. the only fair way to resolve this situation is to determine whether there is a subordinate employment relationship with the platform having to prove that it is not. if we create an intermediate category of protection, as some people want, we will be opening the way to minimal protection and the degradation of labour protection. in the name of the dignity of the world of work and an economy that works for people, we have the right to fight for the rights of these workers.
the report 'fishers for the future' was unanimously adopted by the committee on fisheries. this is a rare event in the european parliament, which shows the broad consensus that has gathered around the seriousness of the situation, giving us the necessary measures to correct it. i thank all my colleagues, and in particular the shadow rapporteurs from all the political groups, for their valuable and constructive contributions. i am particularly grateful to the coordinator of the s&d group in the committee on fisheries, my colleague mrs clara aguilera, for her solidarity and confidence. i am grateful for the many contributions received from the sector, which prove that this issue is understood by all as being decisive for the future of the profession. in 2017, the first social data assessment report in the fisheries sector accounts for around 150 000 people employed in the union fishing fleet, 63% of whom are over 40 years old. these figures confirm what most member states and the frontline partners have been saying for at least two decades. the fishermen are getting older and older, and it is becoming increasingly difficult to attract the new generations to fish. the diagnosis is long overdue, but it takes action to reverse the situation. that is the reason for this own-initiative report. it's time to act. the common fisheries policy aims to ensure the long-term social, economic and environmental sustainability of fisheries. in many communities, the social importance of the fisheries and aquaculture sectors even exceeds their direct economic contribution. in the limited time available to me, i have tried to highlight the most important aspects. improving working conditions, habitability and safety on board are key. despite the european union's accession to international conventions relating to ship safety, the truth is that many of the rules do not apply directly to small-scale vessels, which make up 85% of the community fleet. the need to take account of the specific nature of work on board fishing vessels should also be stressed. it is imperative to enforce the labour law frameworks and to get all member states to ratify ilo convention no 188. improving training and the recognition of training at european level are essential. the recognition of training acquired in different member states is cumbersome and bureaucratic and undermines the right to free movement. the importance of practical knowledge is not lost, but the attraction of new generations of fishermen also depends on formal certification, which helps personal appreciation and social recognition. it is imperative to ensure gender balance and to value the role played by women throughout the fishing industry. only 4% of fishermen in the european union are women. there is a lot to be done to recognize the role of women. to ensure the generational renewal that is essential for the continuity of fishing activity, there needs to be greater social recognition of the activity and its importance for the supply of healthy food, sustainably produced in habitats in good environmental condition and contributing to the health of europeans. we must combat the prejudice that fishermen are predators interested only in exploiting resources without thinking about the future. fishermen are and will increasingly be the guardians of the sea. i call for the report to be adopted by all members next wednesday. i am speaking directly to you, commissioner. it is imperative that the concrete recommendations we put forward are followed up and responded to. this issue has been debated for more than two decades without any concrete action being taken and the situation is of course worse than it was two decades ago. i repeat, commissioner, and i know that i have your support on this matter: it is time to act in favour of the fishermen and the fisheries sector of the european union.
in writing. the application of article 349 of the treaty should take into account the additional social, demographic, economic and climatic challenges faced by the outermost regions. the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the union's policy on the environment and its implementation in the context of the union's policy on the environment. the geographical position of these regions requires the promotion of effective territorial cohesion, with a focus on maritime and air mobility policies that bring these territories closer together and ensure connectivity, minimising the costs of passenger and freight transport and reducing their environmental impacts. the implementation of the green deal must include a blue pact to protect ocean resources and develop maritime potential, and the cap must take account of the specificities of agricultural methods, the protection of biodiversity and the integral and sustainable development of rural areas. promoting sustainable tourism by supporting pilot projects for green and digital solutions is the best path to economic development. the new generations demand better training opportunities, by integrating the reality of the ors into erasmus+, and decent employment, with policies that take into account local economic specificities.
mr president, mr vice-president, mr minister, the science tells us that if we don't act now, we will not be able to limit the temperature increase on this planet to the limits of the paris agreement. fit for 55 will ensure the climate transition and the achievement of the objectives of the climate act. this transition will be cross-cutting and will affect all sectors. today i would like to highlight the transport sector. the legislative proposal on co2 performance standards for new light vehicles will be key to the decarbonisation of this sector. however, we need more action to ensure that the sector does its part to achieve the 2030 target. we will move to zero-emission cars, but along the way we cannot forget to ensure accessibility for our citizens and all regions, including the most remote such as the outermost regions. only with everyone on board will we be able to transition to more sustainable mobility.
madam president, 20 years after september 11 we eloped from afghanistan, committing yet another set of mistakes like we did throughout these years. the us changed the situation on the ground in terms of security for two decades, but now everything has fallen apart. we can only hope that the terrorists don’t find safe haven in afghanistan yet again. but nobody is truly certain of that, because state building was a disaster. the social and economic conditions on the ground are dire. corruption was the norm for many of these years. and we, as europeans, as europe, were always on the weak side of the international coalition, since we always lacked the capacity to determine ourselves, particularly in what relates to security. now, we have a nation destroyed and radicalism back in power. we fear the most for those in afghanistan, particularly women, and the least we can do is pledge that we will do all in our power for this not to be a story of despair for all of them. we need to provide them with some hope. therefore, we must do more; we need to start building the conditions for safe humanitarian aid in afghanistan again, and the conditions for development and equal rights to go back to those people’s lives, to those women’s lives. at the same time we need to reflect on the many mistakes of this 20 years, and on the selfishness of many european authorities and political parties that showed no solidarity towards those refugees that have left that country in despair these last weeks or months. europe must be a land of opportunities, a safe haven for dignity, for human rights, for international law. europe cannot become more and more the land of closed borders and closed hearts. we owe that to the afghans now, and we certainly owe that to ourselves and to the world.
madam president, mr high representative, colleagues, but also to the lebanese people we spoke to on the ground and want to listen to the voice of this european parliament. lebanon faces one of the three worst economic and social crisis of the last century in the whole world. shortage of water, electricity, fuel, decent and affordable food. three out of four people fell into poverty in one year. the world’s highest number of refugees per capita. a default in international payments, a dramatic drop in purchasing power and in salaries. we have seen images of garbage on the streets. hospitals are failing, schools maybe not reopening. this is how bad the situation is on the ground. on top of all of that, a year ago the blast at the port in beirut killed hundreds and displaced thousands. a tragic symbol of the country’s tragedy, of the country’s situation. the perception of corruption and political blockage is everywhere. the situation must change now! humanitarian aid to the population must be a priority of the new government, as well as reforms! long awaited reforms of the political system, of the financial system, of the energy sector, of the judiciary. corruption must stop, inaction must end. the new government has an opportunity if it understands that business as usual is not an option. citizens deserve a government that finally decides, and that works with people’s interests in mind. people cannot wait! we need action now from the new government and the international partners must step-up if that doesn’t happen and sanctions must remain as a possibility if that doesn’t happen. we won’t leave you alone. that’s what we say to the lebanese people. we were there, we saw your despair with our own eyes, and we will not forget.
madam president, the 2020 beirut port explosion was also the explosion of an intricate political system where clan histories and legacies of tragedies, the public and the private, the national and the family, are mixed. a system marked by corruption and impunity that must be brought to an end and that has led the country into the abyss of hyperinflation, unemployment, lack of water, electricity, fuel and medicines and, worst of all, disbelief. the formation of a new government after 13 months of stalemate creates conditions for implementing a reform agenda starting with the agreement with the imf and holding municipal and presidential elections without delay. fair, transparent and inclusive elections. one last chance for all of us. only the lebanese people can choose their future. it is the duty of the european union to support you at this crucial time. i wish that beirut, which nadia tuéni described as "a thousand times dead, a thousand times resurrected", will rise again from the rubble.

on behalf of the ppe group. madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, taxes are one of the pillars of national sovereignty. we must not question this principle of fiscal sovereignty, just as we cannot turn a blind eye to the challenges of a globalised economy and an integrated internal market such as ours. administrative cooperation in tax matters is essential for the effort to combat tax fraud and evasion and money laundering. the exchange of information between member states is a fundamental instrument for the union to be an area of tax justice, where everyone pays what he or she owes and only what he or she owes. this exchange of information must be strengthened by progressively increasing the amount of information available, but above all by improving its quality. what we find in this report is that all the member states, all of them, are failing in this work and that is the main warning we should leave. we need to exchange more information and better information, but first we need to be competent to do what the law already says. let me be very clear: we do not want this enhanced exchange of information to be just an administrative matter, it cannot be indifferent to european citizens. with more and better information we want to reduce red tape, save on administrative burdens and free up resources for the tax authorities to better fulfil their mission, always with the utmost respect for the rights of taxpayers, from data protection to privacy. more information means more responsibility, better information must mean better practice and enhanced cooperation must mean results. that is what this parliament must do and what the citizens of europe deserve.
on behalf of the left group. madam president, the exchange of information and administrative cooperation between tax authorities is essential to combat fraud and tax evasion and money laundering. these objectives are apparently consensual, but every time we discuss one of these reports we find that there are political forces, countries and even european institutions which are not only not willing to do what is necessary but are doing everything they can to prevent these objectives from being achieved with any degree of effectiveness. the fact that we have sought to gather information on the exchange of information and that the same states which have committed themselves to continue and comply with these requirements for the exchange of information refuse to exchange information on the subject is quite worrying, as is the highly restrictive interpretation which the european commission has made of its own constraints, which has allowed this information to escape parliament. if that is the case, and if that is the european commission's interpretation, i hope that in a future review of the cap the european commission will give itself these powers and give parliament access to information to monitor the implementation of the cap. in fact, as has already been said, the european commission has paid very little attention to this area. i also hope that the european commission will welcome the calls made in this report for the expansion of information exchanged between tax authorities, particularly at the level of real estate and dividends, the level of discrimination in information and the level of information from financial institutions on final beneficiaries.

on behalf of the s&d group. mr president, commissioner, we cannot dissociate the recovery from the negative social consequences of the covid-19 pandemic from the european union's long-term employment and social protection goals, which are absolutely vital for the future of the european project and for winning the trust of citizens. those targets are set out in the action plan for the european pillar of social rights, adopted unanimously at the porto summit in may 2021. the regulation on the recovery and resilience facility also sets clear objectives in the social, employment and training fields. moreover, the mechanism requires member states to explain how the national plans will contribute to the implementation of the principles of the european pillar of social rights. it is therefore necessary to clarify how the commission will monitor and ensure that contribution. it is also important to understand how the commission will ensure that the green and digital transition, funded by the recovery and resilience facility, will contribute to reducing poverty and promoting the well-being of europeans. finally, it is important to understand how the commission will ensure that the social reforms included in the recovery and resilience mechanism are consistent with the guidelines of the european semester, in order to avoid contradictions that undermine the credibility of the european project. the title of this question is about reversing the social consequences of the pandemic, but we can't just settle for a reversal. the european union's ambition cannot be to restore the social situation that existed before the pandemic. our mission must be to get out of this crisis with more and better social protection and more and better jobs.

mr president, alongside the climate change crisis, our planet is beset by an unprecedented crisis of biodiversity loss. this is the time to step up our efforts against this crisis and thus achieve the sustainable development goals. the protection of biodiversity must be an integral part of the eu's external policy, particularly in its relations with low- and middle-income countries. funding and technical assistance should be made available to these countries, avoiding further loss of biodiversity that affects everyone. the eu should thus set an example for the rest of the world. special attention should also be paid to our outermost regions and overseas territories which represent around 80% of biodiversity in the european union and are vulnerable to both human and natural influences. by protecting our nature, our biodiversity, we are also ensuring the best use of our planet's resources and a decent future for future generations.

mr president, let me first state that i fully concur with the high representative: there can be no doubt that the us remains the closest and most important strategic partner of the eu. we see several of president biden’s steps as very positive: from the commitment to multilateralism to the rejoining of the paris agreement and the world health organization, to the global agreement on corporate tax under the oecd. however, as much as we point to the positive, we must not take a blind eye to other actions, from the unilateral and non—coordinated withdrawal from afghanistan, to the australia–united kingdom–united states (aukus) agreement in the south pacific. the us acted alone on this, and these are matters of regional and international security that affect us directly. this trend builds from previous presidencies and was at its peak with mr trump. what the us shows us is not that they are not interested in the long alliance with the eu; it is just that they do not depend on the eu. they have their own strategic autonomy. the eu must also have its own. we need to stand prepared to defend our own positions. our shared values with the us do not always converge or translate into common interests. the eu must stand united, speak in one voice and be able to stand for its values and interests in the global arena.
mr. president, let's get to the point: the euro-atlantic alliance continues to prove itself irreplaceable in defending the rules-based world order and in defending the values of democracy and human rights in the face of the rise and increasing assertiveness of the powers and liberals. on the other hand, the withdrawal of the united states from afghanistan without proper coordination with the european union and the aukus agreement without the involvement of the old partners cannot be achieved without any adaptation to change and serious reflection on our part. we should not be naive and should be rather assertive and pragmatic. only a european security and defence policy and greater decision-making capacity in foreign policy will give us effective strategic autonomy and greater effectiveness in action, which will enable us to assert ourselves as a partner with influence on a global scale. the stronger and more assertive europe is, the more relevant it will be to its partners. the diagnosis is made, ladies and gentlemen. we need decisions.
on behalf of the s&d group. – mr president, in less than a month, we’ll be celebrating the fall of the berlin wall. however, regrettable as it may be, new walls have been built to impede freedom to enter some countries. sometimes they are not built with bricks and concrete: they are made out of repression and violation of fundamental freedoms. every one of the thousands of protesters’ detentions, every violation of human rights, every unfair judicial decision is a brick in that wall. belarusian people deserve and expect freedom, democracy and prosperity. the regime of lukashenko does not hesitate to deliver the opposite just to keep him in power, whatever it takes. now he has completely crossed the boundaries of humanity, weaponing human beings by smuggling migrants beyond the belarusian border. this is totally unacceptable. the eu must react, strengthening and broadening the sanctions against those responsible for the state-organised smuggling and the people that continue assuring the survival of the regime. action is needed. additional action is clearly needed from the side of the eu and in the international community. it is clear that lukashenko went too far, but this by no means can justify the way all these migrants are being treated at our borders. the situation on the border between poland and belarus is dramatic, with migrants dying during the cold nights without adequate protection. many, including children, are stuck in limbo for weeks, with others being pushed back. the eu must be clear about what european values are about. yes, saving lives. yes, promoting democracy. yes, promoting human rights in belarus – but also in our own borders.
mr president, this debate is part of the operation of interference and destabilisation against belarus which the european union, its institutions and, in particular, some member states are trying to carry out. the exploitation of refugees and asylum seekers, most of whom come from afghanistan and iraq, who are on the borders of belarus with poland and lithuania, and who are destined for countries that are part of the european union, is a clear example of this action that has no more than geostrategic and political objectives, putting at risk the lives and security of thousands of people who are seeking a better life. what is required is that the european union and the member states should guide their action by respecting the sovereignty, independence and territorial integrity of this country, in compliance with the principles of the united nations charter and international law, and respecting the right of the belarusian people, like any other people, to decide its present and its future.
mr president, one year after the day on which the belarusians took to the streets in protest, there is an endless series of arbitrary arrests, human rights violations, people subjected to torture and forced into exile abroad. to all this we add the hijacking of a plane to detain a journalist, the outlawing of the helsinki committee, the last human rights organization operating inside belarus, and an endless number of immigrant refugees who are being exploited and whose rights are being violated at our borders. commissioner, we need a rapid response to these migrants and refugees that respects international law in its entirety. we must strengthen sanctions against belarus, but sanctions, commissioner, are directed at political actions, and what we are talking about are also crimes, crimes committed by mr lukashenko, and mr lukashenko has to be brought before the international criminal court.
madam president, commissioner, mr president-in-office of the council, the pandemic has led the european union to embark on an unprecedented effort to create a true european health union. this effort includes the creation of hera european health emergency preparedness and response authority, which strengthens our capacity for prevention, detection and response. the objectives we have all set ourselves can only be achieved if we are all equally involved and committed to their achievement. the commission cannot therefore exclude the european parliament from this procedure, because, in addition to the unacceptable legislative precedent, we want to be involved in improving the proposal presented. we need: a real agency and not a structure within the commission; clear governance, with the european parliament as an integral part, not as an observer, but as a full member; robust, clearly defined funding that does not absorb most of the eu4health funds; transparency in its relationship with the private sector; openness in its relationship with patient associations and their representatives; and closeness to our citizens. the european parliament cannot simply approve budgets or wait until 2025 to take stock. after all that has been achieved, we must now complete the construction of the european union for health in the same spirit of collaboration as in the beginning. the europeans will not accept it otherwise.
madam president, commissioner, parliament has a say in the governance of the european union's trust funds. in its workings: parliament will ensure the unity of the budget of the european union. the trust funds shall not act to the detriment of the ivcdci. its management must be transparent. in their use: they are an instrument, that's for sure. but they are an instrument for promoting the values of the european union, human rights, the rights of refugees. they are not a border management tool to be used for the benefit of people, in direct support of their needs, by appropriate bodies, to support people's lives when the first humanitarian aid is no longer required. as regards the turkish mechanism, commissioner, we must continue to be vigilant in respecting the rights of refugees and to ensure that the money actually reaches the projects, the civil society, the people for support in health, education, employment, housing without discrimination.
madam president, commissioner, the pandemic has caused an unprecedented economic and social crisis, hitting the tourism sector hard, particularly in the regions that depend on it the most, such as the autonomous region of madeira, an outermost region. the workers who directly and indirectly contribute to the functioning of this sector have suffered the most. with vaccination reaching around 80% coverage across the european union and the digital certificate in place, there is thus an urgent need to boost a recovery focused on the digital and green transition. the recovery in tourism this summer was positive, but not enough to make up for what was lost. we therefore need a european strategy for tourism and a specific heading in the long-term eu budget that will bring stability and sustainability to the sector and safeguard jobs and overcome the specific challenges faced by businesses, in particular micro, small and medium-sized enterprises.
in writing. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the threats to democracy are of various kinds. harassment, misinformation with obvious intent, use of clearly misleading advertising. these are obvious problems and we see them every day, every time we visit social media. but there are worse things: the promotion of hate speech, the silencing of political opponents, spying, and even interference in electoral acts. this is the reality of the present day, and we must fight against it. in this reality it is clear that self-regulation does not exist, it does not work. we just have to act. i hope that the digital services act package can be a step towards combating this problem. clear and effective legislation, a strategic approach at institutional level with political coordination and credible and robust instruments for offenders are the only alternative.
the increase in energy prices in europe over the past few months is posing an unprecedented challenge to the lives of european citizens. this increase is related to external factors such as the dependence on third countries for energy supply, and not to the climate change, although this has triggered speculation in the market for buying and selling emission allowances. the solution will be to launch a coordinated european response to ensure economic and social cohesion. in addition, it will be necessary to launch a package of social measures to compensate our citizens for the increased burdens they will have to bear, with the aim of reducing energy poverty, a reality that affects millions of europeans. in parallel, the 'fit for 55' legislative package aims to ensure more climate action. in this energy price crisis, improving energy efficiency across the eu will play a crucial role, as will accelerating the transition to renewable energy, to the detriment of fossil fuels, including through additional investment. this will ensure the protection of the most vulnerable groups and at the same time european competitiveness and the climate transition, and specifically the necessary reduction of emissions.
in writing. – the european union must react to the energy price crisis with a set of measures at eu level that member states can implement to face the increases. although the overall gas consumption in europe has decreased from the peak of over 500 million cubic meters in 2010 to less than 380 million cubic meters in 2020, household owners, that account for almost 30% of eu’s energy consumption, are still finding it difficult to switch to new renewable energy sources and over 35% of them still rely on natural gas. ambitious measures must be undertaken swiftly to tackle energy poverty and protect the most vulnerable citizens and businesses. a plan must be set up to better coordinate member states when negotiating gas imports and to provide adequate fast implementable measures such as the ones of the spanish social democratic government. this energy crisis is not a national issue, and should be a reminder that renewable energy is part of our future, thus the solutions must be at eu level.
in writing. portugal is one of the eu countries with the highest energy costs. the causes identified by the european commission, such as increased demand for gas after the resumption of economic activities, low levels of storage or the rise in the price of carbon allowances on the european market, are not convincing. it is clear that the shortage of energy prices is not unconnected to the process of liberalization, privatization and deregulation in the sector that has taken place in recent decades, carried out by successive ps, psd/cds governments with the consent of the eu. the results are visible: in addition to the price increases, employment with rights has decreased, national interests and energy sovereignty have been disregarded, economic, social and environmental problems have been ignored, and the defence of a quality public service has been disregarded. and as the energy deficit and the price of energy increase, so do the profits of the monopoly groups that dominate the sector. energy is a public good and energy supply and affordability is an essential public service. the energy sector, as a strategic sector, is vital to national independence and sovereignty and cannot be subordinated to private interests. it must be at the service of the people and the development of the country, which implies public control.
on behalf of the ppe group. madam president, the pandora papers are not the first revelations about the use of tax havens for illegal or criminal purposes. and if it is true that not all revelations constitute crimes, it is no less true that we may be faced with evidence of tax evasion and money laundering. this suspicion is enough to justify more ambitious and more demanding measures. but unfortunately in the past it was not enough! now that we're here, we have to answer three questions. first, what's wrong? international negotiations within the g20 and the oecd have been dragging on for too long. in the european union we do not use the means we have to the full extent, and in each of our states (and in mine this is blatant), the governments look the other way. they lack not only ambition but also commitment to implementing european legislation. second, what do we have to do? achieve an international agreement on corporate taxation in the era of globalisation and digitalisation. transform the code of conduct and the eu tax havens blacklist into more effective mechanisms. where is the credibility of the list when it ignores jurisdictions like the seychelles, which dropped off the list two days after the pandora papers scandal broke? finally, commit countries to rules on tax transparency and exchange of information that facilitate the fight against tax fraud and evasion. third, when and how? this is the time to decide. we are debating this today in the european parliament and our positions are clear, but it is the member states who have the power to decide. we have no time to lose and the first step is to get governments to the same table. the choice before us is clear, ladies and gentlemen, and it is between the courage to be more ambitious or burying our heads in the sand of some tax haven. we don't want to wait around for the next scandal. we want governments to join us to prevent it.
madam president, commissioner, mr president of the council, ladies and gentlemen, what the pandora papers reveal is very clear: a reality that makes us ashamed, where some choose the rules they follow and the taxes they pay. unfortunately, this is not new. the succession of scandals and the number of tax havens that are multiplying like mushrooms show that the progress made in recent years is not enough. the right of veto of countries in tax matters must be abolished. we have reached the absurd point of having today a veto in the council over one of the protagonists of the pandora papers. and the blacklist of tax havens needs to be reformed, with fairer criteria and more transparency. none of this is news. none of these measures are new. there is a lack of political will in the council. the council lacks the political will to move towards effective legislative instruments for the implementation of a genuine blacklist of tax havens with serious consequences for the states. you can count on us to tighten up the fight against money laundering even further, starting with the creation of a european authority. we cannot, however, afford to be left to the indignation again. here we have a responsibility to fight and to hold accountable those who need to be held accountable to make a difference.

mr president, today, when we discuss revelations of aggressive or harmful tax practices, we come to the inevitable conclusion that something has gone wrong. and if it's important to look back and make that assessment, it's even more essential to look ahead and figure out what we can do differently. the european union is now a single market integrated into a globalised and increasingly digitised economy. the frontiers that the internal market is intended to break down have long since disappeared in the digital market. the opportunities that arise for business must mean more growth, more jobs and more investment. and taxation must be an instrument to serve these aims. it cannot be a blanket of details with holes for tax evasion and money laundering. just as competition must be fair and just, so too must taxation be adequate and transparent. we must use the instruments we have competently and effectively. the code of conduct needs to be better enforced and the blacklist of tax havens needs to have concrete consequences for the jurisdictions it identifies. it is up to each member state to define its taxes, who pays them and how much they pay. the european union has a duty to ensure that cooperation has concrete results in the lives of citizens and businesses. outside, let's speak with one voice. inside, let's be loyal to each other. with more commitment from the member states and more leadership from the union, i am sure we can make progress. it's time to make the first move.
mr president, the pandora papers bring an additional layer of responsibility when we vote on this paramount lalucq report. our workers, families and small businesses are tired of carrying the burden left behind by those who do not pay their fair share of taxes. instead of working together to improve conditions for all, we insist on the tax race to the bottom. to fix this, it is paramount to swiftly implement the oecd agreement and establish a minimum effective level of taxation for corporations. then we must also review the eu list of tax havens. all the words have been said. now we need action and probably even legislation, commissioner. for a proper blacklist, we need a listing process with more transparency and stricter criteria that lists those jurisdictions that refuse to define fair minimum levels of taxation as well. instead of outrage due to one more scandal, what we need is action. this parliament has said repeatedly that, from our side, we are prepared to act.

on behalf of the ppe group. other madam president, the banking union in 2020 faced an unprecedented crisis, but it proved stronger and more resilient than a decade ago. the pillars of supervision and resolution have proven to be well-prepared to ensure the financial stability of the banking sector. and that was essential for us to face the crisis with greater confidence. unfortunately, there is a third pillar which remains incomplete: the european deposit insurance scheme. if there's one thing we've learned from this crisis, it's that interdependence and solidarity are not just words to grace a speech. the delay in this process leaves depositors more or less protected according to the country in which they have their income and savings, and is therefore unjustifiable. and if we are talking about solidarity, we must also talk about credit default swaps. this measure was important to protect citizens in the face of an unexpected crisis. now in some member states the level of claims they check is significant. in my country, for example, the value of the arrears is over 36 billion euros. and if it is true that the priority is the protection of families and businesses, it is no less true that we must look at the size of bad credit, which remains structurally high in many countries. 2020 was a year of crisis in which the banking union proved resilient. let us hope that in a year's time we will be able to make the long-overdue progress. and we'll be here to defend them.

member of the commission. – madam president, first of all i want to thank you all for a very wide-ranging discussion, a discussion that enabled me to hear many aspects and touched on many aspects of the farm to fork strategy, and a discussion that also gave me a sense of the support in the direction that we need to move, but also the concerns. and i was listening very carefully and i will try to address as many of the comments that i heard, although i think i won’t be able to address them all, so i will make an effort. first of all, i just want to say that the targets announced in the farm to fork strategy express our political commitment – i have been saying this from the beginning – and our level of ambition and are set always on the basis of the latest available evidence. i heard the concerns of several meps, of mep de castro, meps aguilera, kuźmiuk, decerle, on the issue to do with the impact assessments. this came up several times. i’m sorry if i haven’t mentioned all the meps that mentioned this. i want to be absolutely clear on this. impact assessments are foreseen for all the major legislative initiatives announced in the farm to fork strategy. and i have repeated this a number of times, and i would really look forward to discussing these with you once they proceed. so this needs to be absolutely clear. there were also several meps – mep lins, mep dorfmann, mep schneider and others – who spoke about why it took so long to publish the jrc study. now the jrc study is not an impact assessment of the farm to fork or the biodiversity strategies. in fact, it explicitly states that it has limitations in the modelling used. for example, it includes only some of the objectives and the actions listed in the two strategies. it was published as soon as possible, but when it became clear that these gaps could not be overcome quickly and that it would take too much time before the models could be improved to address these shortcomings, we decided that the study should be published without further delay because this would enable a discussion with the scientific community in order to improve our modelling capacity, and to be able to better address the critical consumer side of the market. now, at no point have any of us that have stood here – and i have found myself here several times – ever claimed that this transition would be without costs and without the need of adaptation. and of course, the essential question is how we absorb these costs. mep lins and mep dorfmann raised that. and this is why it’s so important that we need to ensure an ambitious implementation of the new cap and put the measures set out in the farm to fork strategy to mitigate risks. i heard all your concerns very carefully and we need the support and to make the most out of the cap and the nextgeneration funds and any other investment at national level in order to support our farmers. it’s very important for the member states to come forward with this and to make this a win-win situation. and overall, the cap strategic plans will present how each member state will achieve the nine specific objectives related to the environment, climate and social dimensions of sustainability. so the cap strategic plans are going to be key in promoting sustainable practices on farms. mep mélin, and i don’t want to let this go because it was almost stated as a fact, mentioned the issue to do with nutri score. at this stage, i’ve said this before, no choice has been made on a future eu harmonised front-of-pack scheme. the choice is going to be based again on a thorough impact assessment, extensive stakeholder consultation and scientific advice, and any proposed scheme needs to be science- and evidence-based. and this is why we have already requested scientific advice from the european food and safety authority (efsa) and the scientific literature review from the jrc. mep sardone and mélin also talked about penalising the mediterranean diets and other products of a healthy diet. we are very aware of the specificities of some agricultural food products and let me assure you that these elements are going to be taken fully into account in our assessment. and i want to touch on what mep schneider said because she spoke about education and this is so important. this is the importance of consumer education, and we need to use front-of-pack nutrition labelling as a useful tool in helping consumers make informed and healthy choices. we need to raise awareness and communication of education in order to help and we will continue to provide financial and administrative support to member states for promoting healthy lifestyles and eating habits. several meps raised the issue to do with a level playing field between third countries and eu producers. yes, and we are deploying all efforts to establish partnerships to move towards sustainable food systems globally so that eu farmers are not disadvantaged compared to third country producers. and we are working on this and i could give examples, but i think i have already gone over my time. and antimicrobial resistance or amr – amr is a huge issue. i call it the silent pandemic. it has become even more apparent with this pandemic of covid-19 that we are going through, and what i can assure you is that – and it was, i think mep polfjärd and chahim – is that we’re going to make every effort to promote a one health approach that protects everyone. addressing amrs through one health continues to be my priority, and we will continue to implement our 2017 action plan against amr. we need to set ambitious amr targets on the farm to fork, and these will be supported by the veterinary medicine regulation. and last but not least, mep guteland raised the issue to do with animal welfare, an issue very close to many of our hearts, and we are proceeding forward with an impact assessment, which has already been launched, on the end the cage age initiative, and we will be taking into account all stakeholders and listening to everyone before we move ahead. and i want to end by saying this: we need to move forward together. we need to be ambitious, we need to be inspired. but most of all, we need to be inclusive and in order to make farm to fork a reality it needs to benefit the consumers, it needs to benefit the farmers, it needs to benefit the producers, and it needs to benefit the environment. and by being inclusive, by talking – and i’m going to always be available to discuss again and again because i heard your concerns – by doing this, i believe that we can move forward and set the eu on the path to a sustainable future.
rapporteur. – madam president, no action is no option. as the intergovernmental panel on climate change (ipcc) and the intergovernmental science—policy platform on biodiversity and ecosystem services (ipbes) have forewarned, if we do not take action now to halt the climate crisis and the loss of biodiversity, this will undoubtedly lead to higher food prices and less food security worldwide. if we want the farm to fork strategy to succeed, the eu must be willing to face up to these challenges and resist pressure from corporate interests to carry on with business as usual. the multinationals, who dominate the seed and pesticides market with manipulated, toxic—dependent products, won’t profit from a change to a toxic-free, resilient form of agriculture. but the farmers, the ecosystems and the consumers will benefit from this transition. those who dominate the food and drink industry, who sell us ultra-processed food and soft drinks, high in sugar, salt and fat, won’t profit from the change to a more healthy food environment, fair and true pricing and crystal-clear labelling. but the farmers, the ecosystems and the consumers will benefit from this transition. those who make profits from confining millions of sentient animals in cramped cages, hauling them hundreds of kilometres to be brutally slaughtered, don’t profit from changes to a more plant-based, healthy diet and crucial changes in animal-welfare protections. but the animals, the caring farmers, the climate, our ecosystems and the consumers will certainly benefit from this transition. we have to listen to science and common sense. we will continue our fight for sustainable food systems, animal rights and animal welfare, healthy diets and a resilient future for farmers. and tomorrow, in the vote on our report, we have a great opportunity to start this journey.
i have to say that i am not in favour of this. madam president, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank you for your work. food is essential for our survival, for our health, for our well-being. food is not only energy, it is also pleasure - especially if it is of high quality. we now have a strategy, and that strategy must - commissioner, you said it - be translated into law. and as soon as we get proposals for that, we expect a proper impact assessment. i also think, after listening to the debate here, that we have not again discussed the strategy 'from the court to the table'. we talked about the court. we always get stuck in farming. we're not talking about the responsibility of industry; we're not talking about the responsibility of commerce. we don't talk much about customer responsibility. if the strategy is called 'from the court to the table', then we should be talking about 'from the court to the table' and not just 'from the court'. we must also stop talking badly about agriculture and farmers on this continent. yes, there are things that need to be improved. but there are also hundreds of thousands of farmers who do their jobs well every day, who know how to produce food and do it sustainably. because i can guarantee you: farmers are the first to think sustainably and to ensure that they pass on their land to the next generation in an orderly way. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. if we do not come up with this know-how, which i sometimes hear in this house, but simply try to go a new way with farmers, then we will ultimately succeed. we will then also encourage young people to take up farming. and if we don't, then everything we do here is a waste of love anyway.
in writing. i agree that the agricultural sector needs to contribute to the union's desire to better protect the environment and ensure a healthier life for all citizens. agriculture has an important role to play, from the quality of food to reducing the amount of pollutants released into the environment. but i don't agree with sacrificing entire industries, firms or jobs to achieve these goals. i cannot support an own-initiative report, such as the 'farm to consumer' report, which imposes numerous restrictions on farmers without having serious studies to identify the effects of these measures and, on this basis, to propose real and appropriate solutions. we already have an energy crisis, largely caused by the unrealism of the "get ready for 55" package. i'm sure no one wants to have a food crisis caused by imposing environmental restrictions without serious consideration. we cannot impose the obligation to reduce pesticides without clear deadlines and transitional periods, we cannot reserve at least 10% of the agricultural area for a highly diverse landscape without compensatory measures, we cannot abruptly reduce the number of livestock farms without ensuring the stability of the supply of the european market.
in writing. this strategy presents an opportunity to ensure food security and to improve lifestyles. as such, we argue that it should ensure: food sovereignty, i.e. the right of states to determine their own food and agricultural systems and the right to produce and consume healthy and culturally appropriate food, recognising food sovereignty as a constitutional right of peoples; the defence of local production and the shortening of production-consumption cycles, as well as the reversal of the liberalisation and deregulation of world trade; the political decision to guarantee small and medium-sized farmers a decent and stable income and their effective participation in an agro-ecological transition; agro-ecological and localised production by small and medium-sized farmers, which respects food and coexists with the natural environment, not using harmful pesticides and chemical fertilizers; the defence of small and family farming and agriculture as using resources in the most balanced way, either through a broad diversification of production or by having a local consumer, even if the average producer is a local consumer, because the potential of the products is fixed. this strategy lacks these essential prerequisites for the implementation of some of its proclaimed principles.

in writing. the position of the polish government and judicial institutions puts the eu in an unprecedented legal, but also existential, dilemma. if implemented in poland, judicial decisions such as the constitutional court's judgment of 7 october will undermine the very foundations of the european project, starting with the treaties, whose ratification presupposes full compliance by the member states. the eu cannot therefore be lukewarm in its response to what is no longer a mere provocation aimed at reaping internal political dividends, but an explicit challenge to european law as a whole and its primacy over national legal systems. if this red line is crossed, the absence of consequences will open the door to discretion and delegitimization of the eu legal order and the european institutions. in view of this, there is no alternative but not to use the recently approved 'conditionality mechanism', in addition to the procedures provided for in the treaties for situations that call into question the rule of law, with the scruple to safeguard the interests of polish citizens, workers and citizens, who should not be punished by the authoritarian misdeeds of their rulers.

in writing. the united nations climate change conference in glasgow, uk (cop 26), to be held from 31 october to 12 november, has the potential to be one of the most significant moments in the history of our fight against climate change. the impact of cop 15, which generated the paris agreement, is well known, but this next conference will aim to move to the implementation phase. to do that, we need more commitment and commitment in this fight and, in particular, in reducing emissions. the other states should also make ambitious nationally determined contributions to the paris agreement's temperature target, which is the only one that will ensure the survival of our planet and future generations. commitments to finance climate change mitigation and adaptation measures should also be stepped up, with particular consideration to be given to low- and middle-income countries that are most vulnerable to the adverse impacts of climate change. only through collective and global efforts will we be able to achieve the decarbonisation of the economy that is intended to be achieved with the current climate and energy transition, with a view to climate neutrality by 2050.

mr president, money laundering is a particularly serious and complex crime. not fighting it is not just attacking the concrete operations, but it is cutting off the financial flow that feeds terrorism, organised crime and networks of trafficking in people, drugs or arms. the european ambition is not justified here simply by the need to recover 1% of the european gdp that is lost. it is motivated above all by the urgency of stopping crimes that attack our values, our core values. the commission's proposals are moving in the right direction, but we need to go further and for that we need the commitment of national governments. we are discussing reform when the current rules are not properly transposed or applied, as has been the case in portugal. a new regulation is a step forward if it strengthens financial market obligations without undermining the competitiveness of firms. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment and the environment from harmful substances. the fight against money laundering requires ambition and realism on the part of all of us. this is not a mere question of law, it is an imperative of the rule of law.
on behalf of the left group. mr president, the oecd agreement on taxation of multinationals is a fraud based on two lies. the first is that multinational companies will be taxed at 15%. that's not gonna happen. the first pillar provides that the first 10 percent of profit margin is exempt, which means that some of the most profitable multinational companies on the planet will pay not 15, not 10, not 5, but 0 percent, and the second pillar has been thrown out for ten years, which is to say, for the calendar, politically speaking. the second lie is that the revenue will be paid in the countries where the economic activity takes place, one of the fundamental principles of the oecd and which is also not respected in this proposal. the share that will be distributed to the countries that are entitled to it corresponds to a small part of the taxed income, which will deepen the inequalities between the developed and the underdeveloped world and between the countries that promote tax evasion and those that want to combat it.
