on behalf of the left group. mr president, in industrial relations there is one party, the employer, who has almost all the power at the outset, and another, the worker, who simply sells his strength. we cannot speak of democracy at work without addressing this relationship which is inherently unequal for workers with precarious and unregulated work ties, instability, low wages, lack of safety conditions, who suffer harassment and repression from their employers at the workplace. democracy is a mirage. the reality is also that the guidelines and policies emanating from the european institutions have not only failed to address these issues but have in many cases aggravated and deepened them. the promotion of democracy at work presupposes the valuing of work and workers, their wages and rights, respect for and promotion of the right to organise and to take collective action and to contract, trade union rights, the right to strike, the principle of the prohibition of unjustified dismissal, the reconciliation of family and personal life with work by means of appropriate working hours, the reduction of working hours in order to enable the worker to participate in political, cultural, trade union and social life. respect for and appreciation of workers, their trade union structures, collective bargaining and contracting must be reflected not just in words but in concrete actions and whenever these rights are violated it will be through the struggle and organisation of workers that they will be won. and it's with them that we'll be.
mr president, commissioner, i would like to pay particular tribute to the rapporteur, my colleague mrs bischoff. the historic struggle of socialists is the struggle for the rights of the workers. the right to strike, to rest or to social protection has eclipsed other rights, which are less revolutionary but which we must not put on the back burner and which are now taking on new importance. workers are interested in and committed to the well-being and productivity of the workforce they are part of and to the sustainability of enterprises. they know the company better than anyone and can promote positive reforms. but for this to happen, they must be properly informed and consulted, as european legislation requires. there is a huge fragmentation of criteria and practices at this level in the european union. that is why we must welcome this report, in particular the call for a revision of the european works council directive and the establishment of a new framework for information, consultation and participation of workers in european companies. it is a path forward that we must take without hesitation.
mr president, mr vice-president, mr minister, at a time of so much concern this parliament must come together and send a clear message from here to the council. we have to unite against external aggressions, like that of vladimir putin or lukashenko, but also unite in fighting this which is the real global war of the covid crisis. we have come together to tell the european citizens that despite the isolated voices that have come here to speak, vaccines really work and i bring the example of my country, the leader in vaccination in europe, was not immune to this new wave of covid. we have the same number of cases as we had a year ago, but a quarter of the critical cases, a quarter of the deaths that we had a year ago in the same period. yes, vaccines work and that is why europe has to come together and the council should launch a debate and a sharing of good practice between member states in the vaccination response. but also to join in helping the global response, the crisis, by getting more vaccines to the world, by exporting more vaccines to developing countries. it's our safety, it's the safety of everyone involved, but it's also our moral duty.
madam president, i think it is very important that in implementing the recovery and resilience mechanism the commission and the council learn from the mistakes of the past, in particular the mistakes that have presided over the troika programmes, and that member states are allowed to develop their own development strategies and to accompany them with the structural reforms that member states and their democratic life determine. i think that this mechanism will have the best chance of success if it is allowed to be decided by the democratic institutions of each country and not by the technocracy in brussels. if mistakes are made, as they already seem to be in my country, in particular the concentration of this support on the usual large companies and not on small and mediumsized enterprises as promised, it will be up to the democratic systems to correct these mistakes and to redirect economic policy towards more productive priorities. the european commission must at least not repeat the mistakes and interferences of the past which have produced such disastrous results.

mr schinas, this is truly one of those interventions which i wish i did not have to make, but which reality forces me to do. how does the european commission respond to the exploitation of migrants and refugees by the dictator lukashenko? it is in line with the same diaphragm, responds by closing borders, suspends the asylum directive and tramples on the european parliament's powers as enshrined in the treaties. what a fine example of democracy, of respect for international law, of respect for human rights! this is not really my europe. we protect those who criminalize ngos that seek to help people who are hungry, cold and who only ask for a chance to live, a chance to survive in safety and with a minimum of dignity, and we deprive the victims of this political game of protection, we deprive them of their right to international protection, we expose them to arbitrary detention, we fail to guarantee them minimum conditions of human rights protection, we fail to guarantee a humane response. this is not my europe, commissioner. my europe would be a europe capable of mobilizing an emergency response to those who are dying of cold and hunger at our borders. this is unacceptable, it does not respect the values which underlie the formation of the european union.
mr president, commissioner, i should like to begin by emphasizing the symbolic value of the european year of rail transport which is now coming to an end. for years we lived under the illusion that highway networks and air travel had made rail obsolete. there was disinvestment and disinterest. it was a profound mistake. climate change and mobility problems show that the train is a transport of the future. it is the most energy efficient and the closest to carbon neutrality enshrined in the european green deal. the railway is an essential element for the decarbonisation of the european transport sector. we acknowledge the support given to the railway sector under the union's extraordinary mechanisms in the context of the pandemic. but we need much more. support must go far beyond circumstantial response to the consequences of the pandemic: it must become structural, sustained and consistent support. a genuine european plan for the development of the railways is urgently needed. long live the train!
mr president, commissioner, what a disappointment to hear the commission explain to us again and again that there is no problem, that this is a matter for the member states to decide, and that, in the end, europe does not have to worry about the torture of these animals in transport, in conditions of purely commercial exploitation and purely recreational, of wild animals in circuses, displaced, trained for the pleasure of a few or a few, and to hear the commission tell us that this is all a matter for the member states! twenty-three member states are legislating; the commission is no longer interested in harmonisation; the fact that we have a shared common legislation is not of interest to the commission; the fact that there are crossborder transport operations is not of interest to the commission; the fact that article 13 of the treaty on the functioning of the european union requires that the welfare of animals, which are sentient beings, be taken into account is not of interest to the commission. so i say to you, commissioner, a million citizens and a european parliament are asking you to act on this issue, and to act effectively.
i would like to thank the commissioner for his reply. imagine living most of your life in a cage. you were probably born in captivity, but you still feel the wildness inside you. you long to walk, swim, and sleep in the open air. but that's not your day-to-day. your daily routine is at the mercy of the people who hold you captive. you were raised in fear of punishment. you had to learn what you had to do to avoid pain. in the evening, in the bright lights, you will be forced to perform, under the shouting, under the laughter of the crowd around you. how can this still happen in 2021? there is nothing cultural, there is nothing educational about dragging wild animals across the continent and abusing them for entertainment. this practice should be a thing of the past in all countries.
mr president, when you deprive wild animals of their freedom, it is man who becomes wild. animals have only one reason to exist: to live, to live freely, and if they have a reason to exist, it is not behind bars or in circuses to satisfy our little amusements and our great resignations in the face of animal condition and suffering. that said, many european countries, under pressure from citizens - because citizens have a head start - have already banned wild animals in circuses. but now it is time for a european-wide ban to harmonise regulations and to ban these ancient practices once and for all. i think that the time has come, in this europe of human rights, to also now found the europe of animal rights.
mr president, commissioner, as already mentioned, more than a million europeans have signed this citizens' initiative. i am very sorry that in your speech you basically stated in an alibi that it was enough to ban the use of animals in circuses in individual member states. i disagree with that and so do the citizens who signed it. for your information, according to activists, there are still over 250 circuses in europe that use, or abuse, as i see it, wild animals. i call for a european-wide amendment, for a european-wide ban. only such a modification can be effective. the current state is not sufficient, that's for sure. so, if you say, 'let's leave it to the member states, the situation is fine,' you are more or less silently supporting the current animal abuse. i beg you to reconsider the situation! the public is interested in the treatment of animals in europe. we're dealing with a lot of things. there's been a citizen's initiative to ban cage-breeding. there are activists who are fighting against the long-distance transport of slaughter animals. europe really has a lot to catch up on. unfortunately, we don't treat animals as we should in europe. it's a strong ethical issue, and we've talked about it repeatedly here on the floor of the european parliament. unfortunately, i sense a reluctance on the part of the commission to address the problems. please, then, let's solve this problem! it's a powerful ethical issue. a europe-wide ban will solve that. we should not rely on the different national laws to solve the problem. it's not like that. so please reconsider your position and propose a europe-wide ban!
madam president, the european union cannot be credible as a leader in environmental protection without offering a dignified life to wild animals. who would believe that we are fighting for the rights of the most vulnerable, if we do not defend the very beings who cannot defend themselves? in captivity, wild animals are tortured exclusively for profit, with no educational or cultural effect, and poor health controls on endangered species undermine conservation efforts. the quality of life of wild animals in circuses is always seriously compromised. it's hard to watch images of bears or tigers circling around, even after they're rescued from their cages. the presence of wild animals in circuses is fuelling the environmental crime we are committed to tackling and poses a serious risk to public health and the safety of staff and the public. 23 member states have already banned all or part of wild animals in circuses and i am proud that, together with my colleagues from the usr, i have contributed to this in bucharest and romania. it is time for the union to take the necessary measures to enforce the prohibition throughout its territory.
madam president, commissioner, in the circus animals suffer from profound physical and emotional abuse. they're not happy animals, and you can tell by the look on their faces. they are slaves to a system and an industry that profits from their suffering. however, in many member states measures have already been taken to ban the use of wild animals in circuses altogether. however, there are countries in the european union which still allow it and others which have only banned the use of certain species and what ends up happening is that circuses travel to member states where these restrictions do not exist. that is why we need a coherent and uniform approach within the community. it is time for the european union to act. it is time to ban the use of wild animals for human entertainment and this time we are debating circuses, but in the very near future we will certainly be debating the ban on cetaceans and other aquatic animals in dolphinariums, as is the bad and terrible example of the zoomarine in portugal.
mr president, memorial has a long history of defending human rights and also, unfortunately, of persecution by the russian authorities. from extortionate fines, arbitrary arrests and prosecutions, to police raids, memorial and those who work with it have been subjected to numerous attempts at silencing and intimidation, also under the infamous foreign agents act, with the ultimate goal of bringing this organization to an end. created with the aim of investigating and documenting russia's repressive past, the memorial has now become yet another victim of the putin government's efforts to rewrite the country's history and, above all, silence and repress all those who oppose it. this is the moment, ladies and gentlemen, to say that we will not let the memory be erased, that we will support the memorial activists, that we will call for stronger sanctions against those who persecute the memorial and that we will help to preserve the memorial archives because this means preserving part of the history of europe.
author. madam president, when maykel castillo received the latin grammys for best urban music and song of the year on november 18, 2021, he did so from the 5 y medio prison in cuba, and not from a stage in las vegas. the song patria y vida, which won him the award, playing on fidel castro's slogan patria o muerte is precisely a message of liberation for the cuban people. as with maykel, the oppression of artistic and civic movements and the gag order imposed by the cuban government have been a constant in recent months and are unacceptable. no artist should be imprisoned for his art. this blackface of the last few months in cuba, the attack on human rights, must be reversed and the political dialogue and cooperation agreement with cuba can also be used for this purpose. however slow progress may be, or slower than we would like, breaking off the lines of dialogue would not help cuba. in cuba, as in other latin american countries - and i had the opportunity to see this recently in venezuela - it is through inclusive political dialogue that we will be able to make progress and not through sanctions that worsen the daily lives of citizens. and i would say, moreover, that they often have the effect of uniting them against those who sanction them, rather than revolting against those who restrict their rights and undermine the credibility of their institutions.
i am not going to go into the details of the matter, but i would like to say that i am very pleased that the commission has taken the initiative to put forward a proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos. those are three very clear messages. the first message is to lament, denounce, criticize the authoritarian response that the cuban government continues to give to any expression of dissent, whether from the civic space, whether from the artistic world or from any minimally political articulation of the opposition. the cuban government must respect the right to peaceful demonstration, the right to freedom of expression, and i think the cuban government would be wrong if it thought that, since the 15m march did not take place, then nothing has happened here. no, it does happen: there is frustration, there are economic and social hardships that lead to this expression of discomfort. secondly, when this parliament passes a resolution on cuba, it is not balanced if it does not include a criticism and a condemnation of the american blockade, which has repercussions on the socioeconomic situation of the cuban people. and thirdly, we also believe that the political dialogue and cooperation agreement between the european union and cuba is the framework, the space, where we can continue to try to help the cuban people on the road to greater freedom and democracy.
on behalf of the ppe group madam president, we have a moral debt to those who defend the freedom of their people with their lives and who only want the values and freedoms that we here enjoy. two sakharov prize laureates, berta ramos and guillermo fariñas, have addressed this house, its president and mr borrell, to ask for the protection of our european union against the persecution of a regime which continues to fail to respect minimum conditions; in this house, the european parliament, that struggle has been recognised and with the vote of all the groups we have awarded the sakharov prize. disillusioned with the union's support, the laureates denounce the neglect and make accusations, in particular against mr borrell and the european external action service; their desperate situation is such that they propose the return of the prize as a form of protest. i am here publicly calling on berta ramos and guillermo fariñas not to renounce this prize, awarded by the true elected representatives of the european citizens in this parliament; and they will do so, they will talk to mr borrell and the european external action service. the last two resolutions passed in this house highlight the harsh reality faced by the laureates: since 2016, when the political dialogue and cooperation agreement was signed, the whole situation has worsened: torture continues, the persecution of citizens, and, moreover, the regime's organizations are being funded from the european union; none of these organizations have been funded, which have even been awarded the sakharov prize. what has to happen in cuba for mr borrell, the european commission and everyone to wake up to the persecution, the torture, of this bloodthirsty regime? i wonder where we will get the courage, the character, to point out a dictatorship for not respecting human rights when even here we refuse to congratulate christmas; how the far left, the previous deputy, is offended, right?: defending a torturing regime, but, nevertheless, offended because they celebrate christmas. how can we get the courage to defend that? that's the question that remains. merry christmas to all, and especially to the cuban prisoners.
on behalf of the ecr group. – mr president, for the third time, despite covid restrictions, we refer to urgent procedures to expose atrocities by the cuban communist regime against its own people, not to speak about full debates when we discuss all issues. yes, we do have agreement with cuba, but to provide argumentation to one of the authors, there are no common values, no common principles, between us. there are deep systemic differences and we have to act quickly, decisively and resolutely to defend people, to impose sanctions and to prevent spreading of these kinds of policies, a kind of stalinist policies, to all other parts of the latin-american region.
on behalf of the left group. mr president, we are talking about cuba again today, for the third time in the last six plenary sessions of this parliament. it illustrates well how the instrumentalization of alleged human rights violations can be used as a weapon of interference. the name of the debates and the titles of the resolutions may change, but the objectives are the same as always. on the one hand, they are calling into question the achievements, the development options, the sovereignty and independence of cuba and are interfering in the process of normalizing relations between the european union and cuba in the dialogue and cooperation agreement, and on the other hand, they are trying to conceal the existence of the blockade imposed by the united states of america, which, in defiance of the principles of the united nations charter, brutally disrespects and violates the rights of the cuban people. we call for dialogue and cooperation between the european union and cuba on the basis of equal rights, without interference and on the basis of mutual respect. we therefore demand once again an end to the criminal blockade. we express our solidarity with cuba and its people and praise their example of sovereignty, resistance and struggle.
